| Real alternatives, authority, information, and burden distribution affect action appraisal | `C` | Supports role-bound option fields and capacity calibration |
| \(D_0\) dignity is untouchable / non-negotiable rather than a score of maturity | `C` | Supports the Chapter 16 dignity firewall and the safe use of its Adult Orientation shorthand |
| Role- and trajectory-sensitive enactment analysis | `C` | Precedent for temporally and relationally bounded comparison |
| “Adult Orientation” as a VECTOR-new supporting shorthand for corrigible consequence-carrying praxis | `N` | Label is new, but Chapter 19 reduces it from independent construct to supporting shorthand; not a MIP maturity score or person type |
| The Chapter 16 stance / Adult Orientation shorthand does not alter \(D_0\) | `R` | VECTOR application of the MIP dignity boundary |

The audit rule for the supporting shorthand is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Adult Orientation shorthand}
\neq
AdultPerson.
}
\]

VECTOR uses MIP to constrain misuse, not to inherit an anthropology of the whole person or to restore independent theoretical status to the label.

They do not collectively contain VECTOR in latent form.

| Abstract possibility does not create universalized responsibility | `C` | Blocks retrospective and possibilist responsibility inflation |
| No retroactive guilt from later-recognized possibility where effective capacity was absent | `C` | Supports temporal discipline in option and Ought claims |
| Imagining a scenario does not itself generate responsibility | `C` | Supports `conceivable ≠ capacity` |
| Θ can accumulate costs and close windows | `C` | Timing, action-corridor, and missed-window pressure |
| Ψ should not be externalized from self-binding into automatic binding of others | `C` | Supports self-/other-binding boundary |
| General VECTOR timing architecture | `R` | Reconstructs these pressures through timing, option status, reversibility, and cost topology; derived DIST may summarize orientation–enactment separation |
| `OUGHT_account` / `OUGHT_constraint` | `N` | Not ANTICIPATION claims |

The key audit boundary is:

\[
\boxed{
later\ possibility\ recognition
\neq
earlier\ effective\ capacity.

This section states positively what PMS–VECTOR introduces rather than searching retroactively for approximate precursors.

### Core architecture

| Geometry Firewall / `VECTOR ≠ vector space by default` | `N` |

| Claim | Status |
|---|---:|
| DIR as a generalized bounded directional claim type | `N` |
| Trade-Off / Incomparability / Non-Capture as distinct VECTOR outputs | `N` |
| ROT as a general perspective-testing and discovery component | `N` |

The rule governing this section is intentionally simple:

\[
\boxed{

}
\]

Novelty does not weaken VECTOR. Unclear provenance would.

---

## F.9 Claim Dependency Map

Provenance asks:

> Where does the claim come from?

Dependency asks:

> What else must weaken, change, or disappear if a parent claim fails?

The governing rule is:

\[
\boxed{
\text{Dependency transmits vulnerability, not automatic warrant.}
}
\]

A child claim may depend on a parent. The existence of several children does not thereby provide several independent warrants for the parent.

### Robust Orientation

A result of Robust Orientation depends at minimum on:

\[
DIR
+
RelevantROT
+
RivalPressure
+
ProvenanceAudit
+
NonCompensation
+
\Lambda\ preservation.
\]

A compact dependency representation is:

```text
RobustOrientation
├── typed DIR
├── materially relevant ROT
│   ├── declared change
│   ├── declared constants
│   ├── reference continuity
│   └── loss capacity
├── serious rival pressure
├── provenance / echo audit
├── non-compensation
├── no unresolved claim-critical regression
└── preserved residue
```

If materially relevant ROT is missing, the result need not become false. It may reduce to:

\[
BoundedOrientation.
\]

If reference continuity fails, the result may become:

\[
NonCapture
\]

or a new bounded claim.

If the rival defeats the baseline, the claim may require revision or collapse.

Dependency therefore determines **how to reduce**, not merely whether to reject.

---

### `OUGHT_account`

The dependency map must distinguish baseline PMS-LOGIC attributability from VECTOR's orientation-mediated uplift.

\[
\boxed{
BaselineAttributability_{LOGIC}
\neq
\Delta Attributability_{orientation}.
}
\]

For the latter, `OUGHT_account` depends on:

\[
RelevantOrientation
+
ExternalPremiseSupport
+
ActorUptake
+
EffectiveCapacity
+
ConsequenceRelation
+
Role/TimeConditions.
\]

Operationally:

```text
OUGHT_account(R) / orientation-mediated uplift
├── R is relevantly oriented under bounded DIR/ROT pressure
├── material external premises are supported
├── orientation is actor-accessible and understood enough for contest/response
├── actor/role has relevant effective capacity
├── consequence relation connects capacity to subsequent praxis
└── role and scene-time conditions keep the relation materially live
```

If uptake is missing, VECTOR cannot claim that **its orientation** increased attributability merely because the analyst stated the claim. This does not erase a baseline LOGIC relation independently supported by capacity and consequence.

If effective capacity falls away, the orientation-mediated account claim must be reduced or retyped. If only visibility remains, `Visible(R)` does not preserve the uplift. If an external premise or consequence relation is defeated by a rival explanation, the practical attribution claim must weaken accordingly.

---

### `OUGHT_constraint`

\[
OUGHT_{constraint}(G,R_G)
\]

depends on:

\[
OperativeG
+
ExternalPremiseSupport
+
EffectiveOptionField
+
ConstraintRelations.
\]

Operationally:

```text
OUGHT_constraint(G, R_G)
├── G is actually operative
├── material factual premises about G are supported
├── effective option field is correctly typed
├── response class R_G is non-empty
├── each member of R_G satisfies the relevant G-condition
├── maintainer / modifier / claimed authority are visible
├── burden bearers / exit / alternative carriers are visible
├── evidenced transformation routes are considered
└── legitimacy remains external unless separately warranted
```

If an invisible but real option is discovered, \(R_G\) changes. If \(G\) is revised, the old constraint claim does not simply persist. If system-level carriage is real but the bearer assignment is unsupported, the system requirement may remain while the person-level claim collapses.

\[
\boxed{
SystemCarriageRequired
\neq
PersonXMustCarry.
}
\]

---

### Tragic Constraint

Tragic Constraint depends on a valid `OUGHT_constraint` configuration plus:

\[
\forall o\in R_G:
ResidualLoss(o)
\]

and the bounded evidential claim that no currently evidenced effective transformation is available within the declared scene-time window, capacity field, and dependency structure.

```text
TragicConstraint
├── valid operative G relation
├── effective option field correctly typed
├── residual loss across R_G
├── mechanism audit for second-order transformations
├── capacity / authority / dependency / window audit
└── no currently evidenced non-lossful route in the declared field
```

If a previously invisible no-loss option is real, tragedy falls. If Second-Order Agency can create or open such an option in time, tragedy falls. If a rival shows that the field was misdescribed, tragedy falls. A merely imagined rescue route does not by itself change current option status.

\[
\boxed{
\text{tragic constraint is falsifiable by evidenced possibility, not by imagination alone}.
}
\]

---

### Corrigible Consequence-Carrying Praxis / “Adult Orientation” Shorthand

The Chapter 16 stance does **not** depend on choosing the substantively correct outcome. Its content is already carried by lower-level relations:

```text
CorrigibleConsequenceCarryingPraxis
├── bounded relevant orientation
├── preserved corrigibility
├── willingness to carry relevant consequence
├── awareness of capacity limits
├── preserved Λ / residue
├── handling of orientation–enactment source conditions without reifying derived DIST
├── recognition of distributed agency where relevant
└── no conversion of orientation into authority over others
```

A specific choice can later prove wrong while the stance remains corrigible. Conversely, a substantively successful choice does not establish the described stance if the result depended on unrotatable certainty, hidden cost externalization, or authority inflation.

Chapter 19 shows that the dependency map remains intact when the *Adult Orientation* label is deleted. The label therefore carries no independent dependency node and remains supporting shorthand only.

---

### VECTOR Standalone Claim

The strongest dependency map concerns VECTOR itself.

The standalone theory claim depends on additional discrimination beyond what existing PMS structures already supply.

```text
StandaloneVECTOR
├── DIR adds discriminative purchase
├── ROT adds more than ordinary reframing
├── prior DIST core claim has been genuinely reduced rather than protected
├── Adult Orientation has been reduced to supporting shorthand after deletion testing
├── option-status architecture discriminates real from illusory possibility
├── second-order agency adds field-transformation discrimination under mechanism evidence
├── cost topology adds non-fungible consequence discrimination without speculative inflation
├── convergence / closure machinery produces real claim loss and bounded stop
├── specialist rivals can win specialist burdens without assimilation
├── same-burden non-PMS rivals can reduce DIR / ROT / standalone scope (E.23)
├── Ought typology prevents rather than causes normative conflation
└── remaining DIR–ROT core can still be reduced if these contributions fail
```

If every major component becomes losslessly reducible to pre-existing PMS terms:

\[
\boxed{
StandaloneVECTOR
\rightarrow
Reduction.
}
\]

If only one component fails, component reduction may be sufficient.

If the theory works only in some domains, domain non-capture or scope reduction may be appropriate.

If a rival architecture provides the same or better discrimination with less complexity, rival superiority may justify stronger reduction. E.23 already enacts that branch for calibrated commensurable multi-criteria orientation, narrowing the domain in which DIR (Direction)–ROT (Rotation) can claim primary standalone discrimination.

The dependency map therefore closes the appendices where Chapter 19 placed the burden:

\[
\boxed{
\text{A theory of orientation must be capable of orienting away from itself.}
}
\]

Appendix F does not add a final argument for VECTOR. It makes visible what would have to change if VECTOR's arguments fail.

That is the intended function of provenance and dependency: not to make the theory harder to remove, but to make its removal, reduction, or revision traceable.
